- **Type**: positions-evolve
- **File**: obsidian/positions/moral-status.md
- **Notes**: **Recorded, not registered, and the reason is length.** The 2026-09-06 pass added the venue and restated the necessity upstream, and changed *Asserts* from "the rival to beat is **biocentrism**" to "the **registered** rival to beat" — one word, the honest one, because the roster is incomplete. What it could not afford is the rival itself. **Measure before acting** (`tools.curate.length.analyze_length`, positions thresholds 1500 / 2500 / 4000): at mint `positions/moral-status` = **2,499** (hard 2,500, one word of headroom) and `positions/moral-status-calibration-history` = **2,357**. A `Would shift if` limb naming the rival costs ~8 words and its reply costs more again, so **this task cannot be done without length relief first** — either a condense of [P-MS1](/positions/moral-status/#p-ms1)'s *Asserts* (the Varner hostile-witness parenthetical and the Vulcan limb are both recent outer-review fixes: do not regress them) or a third companion file. A two-entry domain file at its hard ceiling also cannot take a P-MS3 at all, which is the larger operator question.
  **The rival.** Kagan, *How to Count Animals, more or less* (2019), defines agency behaviourally and argues from a robot planet (p. 28: "It simply doesn't matter to me that the child and its mother are 'mere' robots, lacking in sentience") that agency suffices for standing without sentience; Mogensen, "Once More, Without Feeling" (*PPR* 111(1), 2025, doi:10.1111/phpr.70018), generalises this into a pluralism on which autonomy can confer moral status without welfare subjectivity. Both are already cited in [the-experience-requirement-on-well-being](/topics/the-experience-requirement-on-well-being/) §"A Second Rival to Necessity", which is the venue [P-MS1](/positions/moral-status/#p-ms1) now lists — take the bibliographic forms from there and re-verify at Crossref before writing.
  **Do NOT overstate the reply.** The Map's reply territory is (a) Mogensen's own diagnosis — "What does the work in this thought experiment is surely the attribution to that robot of a kind of mental state that corporations saliently lack …: namely, emotion" (2025a) — so stripping the pleading removes the intuition and returns the case to the affect question; and (b) that on views where autonomy entails phenomenal consciousness the pluralism collapses into sentientism. Neither is a refutation: Mogensen grants "at least modest credence" to unconscious emotion, which routes the dispute straight back into the unconscious-affect draw [valence](/concepts/valence/) records as conceded from inside both sides' commitments. Write it as a live rival with a located reply, on the pattern the biocentrist limb already uses ("locates that disagreement rather than settles it"). **No band should move on this** — adding a known rival to the roster records exposure the entry already had; say so rather than re-rating.
  **Dated note goes in `positions/moral-status-calibration-history.md`, not the register** (that domain's convention since 2026-09-05). If the limb lands, check whether [positions](/positions/)'s Domains gloss for this domain ("biocentrism named as the live rival on the necessity direction") needs re-deriving — `positions/positions.md` is itself 2,698 words against the same 2,500 advisory, so prefer a length-neutral swap.

### ✓ 2026-09-06: Write article on the coupling-engagement condition — the construct now bearing the Tenet-4 defence in two articles has no page
- **Type**: expand-topic
- **Notes**: **The sharp on/off subject-fact is now grounded in two articles on whether the mind–brain coupling "engages at all" — described as "a simple, non-graded relation that either holds or does not" — and the corpus has no article about that condition.** `concepts/phenomenal-sorites-problem` §"The Fact-Maker Problem" installed it as the truth-maker (declining Sorensen's truthmaker-gap route and the graded-physical-property route), and the 2026-09-06 deep-review of `concepts/is-conscious-being-a-natural-kind` re-grounded that article's on/off joint onto the same construct, off `interface-threshold`.
  **Grep-verified 2026-09-06**: the only live pages discussing the coupling's *engagement condition* (as against its *mode* or its *efficacy*) are `concepts/universal-coupling-response`, `concepts/panpsychism`, `concepts/degrees-of-consciousness` and `phenomenal-sorites-problem` itself. The two apparent hosts are about something else — `concepts/coupling-modes` covers *how* influence works (basis / timing / probability control; 3 matches for "engag"/"adequa" in the whole file) and `concepts/interface-threshold` is, by both wing articles' own account, an *efficacy* boundary with phenomenal consciousness present on both sides (1 match).
  **What the article must actually do — state a live seam, not resolve it by fiat.** `phenomenal-sorites-problem` closes by volunteering the objection: "if the coupling's engagement is itself triggered by a graded physical variable, the sorites re-enters at the trigger's switch-on point." Meanwhile `concepts/universal-coupling-response` states the Map's coupling condition (architectural adequacy) and says of it: "The boundary between 'adequate' and 'inadequate' architecture need not be sharp … there is no precise moment when a developing embryo's nervous system becomes adequate … The vagueness is in the boundary, not in the coupling." **Neither article cites the other in either direction (0 hits both ways, both trees).** The optimistic review deliberately did **not** adjudicate whether that final clause holds — that is a pessimistic-review or deep-review job, and `universal-coupling-response` is outside the reviewed wing, so no task was minted against it. The new article's job is to set the engagement condition out as a subject: what distinguishes engagement from mode and from selection-efficacy, what could make engagement non-graded while adequacy is graded, and what would have to be true for the trigger problem to bite. Write it as an open seam with the Map's candidate answers, in the manner `concepts/ontic-structural-realism` handles its own composite — not as a resolution.
  **Section**: `concepts/`, ~2200w. Capacity measured live with `tools.evolution.state.count_section_files`: concepts **324/360**. Host considered and rejected: `concepts/phenomenal-sorites-problem` states the problem and is the one file in the wing that categorically cannot take it — 3439w, `soft_warning`, **61 words from its hard ceiling** (measured live). `concepts/is-conscious-being-a-natural-kind` has 88 words to soft and is in any case about the natural-kind machinery.
  **Tenets**: **No Many Worlds** (Tenet 4) primarily — the determinate subject-fact indexical identity fastens to; **Dualism** and **Minimal Quantum Interaction** secondarily, noting `phenomenal-sorites-problem`'s care that the minimal-interaction tenet "concerns influence rather than presence."

### ✓ 2026-09-06: Research structuralism about phenomenal character — five live pages defer to a rival that has no page
- **Type**: research-topic
- **Notes**: **Grep-verified absent 2026-09-06** across `topics/`, `concepts/`, `voids/`, `apex/`, `positions/` and `research/`: the strings "phenomenal structuralism", "structural qualia", "neurophenomenal structuralism", "Loorits" and "Lyre" occur in only two live articles (`concepts/ontic-structural-realism`, `concepts/galilean-exclusion`) plus `voids/interface-formalization-void`, and in **no research note on the subject**. Yet five live pages point at it and hand it the argument: `concepts/ontic-structural-realism` ("phenomenal structuralism does the extending work, so that is where the Map's argument belongs"), `concepts/intrinsic-nature` ("a separate opponent with its own literature"), `concepts/galilean-exclusion`, `topics/primary-secondary-quality-boundary` and `voids/interface-formalization-void`. The Map thus has a developed treatment of ontic structural realism — which by its own finding threatens the Map's *route*, not its thesis — and none of the rival that threatens **Tenet 1** directly.
  **Primary target, and the reason this is research-first rather than expand-first: Fink, S. B., Kob, L. & Lyre, H. (2021), "A structural constraint on neural correlates of consciousness," *Philosophy and the Mind Sciences* 2, https://doi.org/10.33735/phimisci.2021.79.** `concepts/ontic-structural-realism` §"What Went Unread" records that "the stronger formulation of phenomenal structuralism originates there and goes unquoted" — writing the article without it would repeat the secondhand-characterisation problem that article discloses. Also retrieve **Loorits (2014)**, *Frontiers in Psychology* 5:237 (partly quoted already: qualia "are in fact compositional and have an internal structure"; consciousness "misperceives some of its own highly complex structural properties as monadic and qualitative") and **Lyre (2022)**, *Neuroscience of Consciousness* 2022(1) niac012 (quoted: "the qualitative characters of sensory experiences are relational rather than intrinsic properties"). Note Lyre's declared non-structural input — "a solution of the Newman problem of the brain in terms of spatiotemporal relations" — and Loorits's stated testable commitment, that inverted qualia are not ideally positively conceivable.
  **Chains to** an `expand-topic` for a `concepts/` article (~2400w). Capacity measured live with `tools.evolution.state.count_section_files`: concepts **324/360**. Host considered and rejected for in-place expansion: `concepts/ontic-structural-realism` is 2655w `soft_warning` (155 over soft) and its whole point is that it concerns the ontology of *physics*, so absorbing this would blur the distinction it exists to draw; `concepts/intrinsic-nature` is 2422w with 78 words to soft.

### ✓ 2026-09-06: `concepts/degrees-of-consciousness` opens "Consciousness is not binary" and qualifies it 180 words later — truncation-hostile, and the missing reciprocal to the natural-kind article
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/degrees-of-consciousness.md
- **Notes**: **Two small items on the same file, both measured live 2026-09-06 with `tools.curate.length.analyze_length` (concepts 2500/3500/5000 printed from `THRESHOLDS`): 2329w, `ok`, 171 to soft, 1171 to hard — the file can afford both.**
  **(1) Lead ordering, not content.** The body's first sentence is the bare "Consciousness is not binary." The qualification that keeps the article consistent with the Map's sharp on/off position arrives at the *end of the same ~180-word paragraph*: "what is graded throughout is what a subject *has*, while whether a subject is present at all is a further question, left open here, on which [the moral-status threshold](/topics/moral-status-threshold-or-degrees/#presence) rests." Under the style guide's LLM-first truncation-resilience rule, a fetch that truncates mid-paragraph takes away the unqualified claim — which is the *opposite* of what `concepts/phenomenal-sorites-problem` and `concepts/is-conscious-being-a-natural-kind` hold about the on/off fact. Fix by **reordering, not adding**: bring the contents-versus-presence qualifier forward to the first or second sentence. The qualification is already present and correct — this is a presentation defect, not a substantive error, and there is nothing to re-argue.
  **(2) Reciprocal Further Reading bullet.** Measured both directions on frontmatter-stripped bodies this run: `concepts/is-conscious-being-a-natural-kind` cites this article by name for exactly the contents/presence distinction ("as [degrees-of-consciousness](/concepts/degrees-of-consciousness/) explores"), and this article's Further Reading lists `phenomenal-sorites-problem` but **not** the natural-kind article — which is the wing's other statement of the same distinction. Add one Further Reading bullet pointing to `concepts/is-conscious-being-a-natural-kind`. A Further Reading bullet rather than an inserted body sentence, per the standing finding that outbound cross-link sentences get written into neighbours and read by nobody.

### ✓ 2026-09-06: `topics/ethics-of-consciousness-invertebrate-question` §The Dualist Difference (L45, L51) says physicalism grades moral status continuously with neural complexity — false as stated (Singer, Sebo, Wendler and Rethink Priorities are physicalist unitarians) — and L51's "presence of a subject is binary" is unreconciled with `concepts/degrees-of-consciousness` L36
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/ethics-of-consciousness-invertebrate-question.md
- **Notes**: Flagged twice on 2026-09-05 and minted by neither: the 11:05 research-topic run (changelog entry `2026-09-05T11:05:00+00:00 - research-topic`, "Flagged for downstream tasks (not actioned)") and [moral-status-gradualism-and-the-threshold-view-2026-09-05](/research/moral-status-gradualism-and-the-threshold-view-2026-09-05/) §Assess-First Verdict + §Potential Article Angles 7(a); the 16:25 refine of this same file (changelog `2026-09-05T16:25:09+00:00 - refine-draft`, the Gemini §IV aggregation pass) left this paragraph untouched. **Both loci re-grepped verbatim from disk at mint time** (line numbers from 2026-09-05; re-read before editing): **L45** "Physicalist ethics of animal consciousness typically proceeds through graduated attribution. Organisms with more complex neural architectures are assigned higher probability of consciousness, and moral consideration scales accordingly. A chimpanzee gets more moral weight than a bee, which gets more than a nematode, in a smooth gradient that reflects neural complexity." **L51** "**The threshold matters more than the gradient.** Under physicalism, moral status increases continuously with neural complexity." The second is false as a claim about physicalism: Singer's equal consideration, Sebo 2025's expected-value principle, Wendler 2023 (*Life Without Degrees of Moral Status*), Rachels and the Rethink Priorities Moral Weight programme are all physicalist **unitarians** — physicalism does not entail gradualism, and the Map's own [moral-status](/positions/moral-status/) [P-MS2](/positions/moral-status/#p-ms2) names Kagan 2019 (hierarchy) and DeGrazia 2008 (unequal consideration) as the rivals of record precisely because the field is split. **Fix both lines together** so the section's contrast survives: what physicalism supplies is graded *credence* about consciousness and graded *stakes* (welfare ranges); whether *status* is graded is the separate unitarian/hierarchy dispute, and dualism's contribution is to relocate the status question from complexity to coupling architecture — L47 already says this, so the repair is mostly deletion and re-attribution, not addition. L45 can keep "graduated attribution" if it is attributed to credence rather than to status. **Second locus, same pass**: L51's "the presence of a subject is binary in a way that physicalist accounts avoid" sits unreconciled with [degrees-of-consciousness](/concepts/degrees-of-consciousness/) L36 "Consciousness is not binary" (and its L122 use of Tenet 5 against on/off treatment). The reconciliation the research note supplies is degrees of *what a subject has* versus the *presence of a subject*; state it in one clause here with a link to degrees-of-consciousness, and do not edit degrees-of-consciousness unless a single reciprocal clause is needed (that file was edited 2026-09-02; keep any touch minimal). Do NOT re-run the enhancement discriminator case or import the forthcoming moral-status article's argument — link `positions/moral-status` [P-MS2](/positions/moral-status/#p-ms2) instead; the `topics/moral-status-threshold-or-degrees` expand minted this run will cross-review this file when it lands. Keep the link and the hedge when rewriting L45/L51. ### ✓ 2026-09-06: `topics/valence-as-a-natural-kind` — the lead attributes to three reviewers a conflation none made (one already named the currency), the 2025 constitution reading is glossed into elimination the source does not license, and L79 breaks the Occam symmetry L87 headlines
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/valence-as-a-natural-kind.md
- **Notes**: All findings verified on disk 2026-09-06 against the three source reviews, the `[raw]` research-note extracts, the register and the sibling articles. **The article's philosophical architecture is sound and its central relocation is right — every fix below either narrows an over-claim or makes the conclusion better founded. Do not weaken the asymmetry result.** Length is **2993w against topics 3000 soft / 4000 hard**, so this pass must be **length-neutral or negative**: parts (1), (3) and (5) all shorten, which buys room for (2), (4) and (6).
  **(1) HIGH — THE LEAD MISDESCRIBES ALL THREE REVIEWS (L29, L31).** L29 opens "The objection … runs two questions together." Checked: **Claude Opus 5 2026-09-04 L108** is a conditional plus "**Not engaged**" — it asserts nothing about what Barrett holds and commits no conflation. **Claude 2026-09-05 L66/L130** flags that Barrett's constructed emotion is *absent from the register entirely*, alongside Frankish, Dennett and Berridge–Kringelbach — a coverage complaint, and a correct one (the research note's Corpus Baseline confirms zero corpus engagement). **ChatGPT 5.6 Pro 2026-09-04 L343** reads "valence may be distributed, context-dependent or conceptually constructed rather than a unitary primitive **currency**" — ChatGPT's target *is* the currency, so L31's "the Map's real exposure sits one position to the side of where the objection aimed" is **false for that reviewer**. Rewrite both sentences: the two-questions distinction is the Map's own clarification of a literature that keeps the questions apart, not a correction of the reviewers; and the currency result **corroborates** ChatGPT's formulation rather than redirecting it. Two sentences rewritten in place, length-neutral.
  **(2) HIGH — THE 2025 DICHOTOMY OMITS THE READING THE SOURCE SUPPORTS, AND "no felt residue" IS NOT IN THE SOURCE (L63, L67).** The `[raw]` 2025 quotation reads "… **These are the qualities of felt experience.** They are not properties of the intrinsic brain signals alone or of the incoming sensory signals alone but of their relations." That affirms felt-ness and denies only that the qualities belong to the signals *alone*. Make it **three** readings: (i) *implementation* (as now); (ii) **relational constitution of a genuinely felt quality** — phenomenal relationalism, which the quoted text most directly supports and which **leaves [P-VS3](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs3) its relatum**, since "intrinsic" in [P-VS3](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs3) modifies *value*, not the relatum's mode of constitution; (iii) *eliminative compression* — "no felt residue underneath", which is the **article's own extrapolation and appears nowhere in the retrieved 2025 body**, and which converges on illusionism, already booked as [P-VS3](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs3)'s live defeater. Then **re-target the transferred reply at (iii) only**: the registered reply at `concepts/evaluative-phenomenal-character` (quoted accurately — "The formalism specifies how valence is computed, weighted, and accessed. It does not specify why the computation should be *felt*") answers a *computational* defeater, and a relationalist who says the felt quality *is* the relation has not skipped the felt-ness step. Finally **downgrade L67's "a serious countermodel stands against it unrefuted"** — L65 already concedes Barrett & Theriault 2025 was not obtained, and the research note's Angle 3 deferred this study until it is read. State it as a possible extrapolation of an unobtained ontology converging on an already-registered defeater.
  **(3) HIGH — L79 COMMITS THE INFERENCE L53 BLOCKS, IN THE SENTENCE THAT RESTATES THE BLOCK.** L53: "Reading 'no dedicated module' as 'no kind' imports the essentialist standard Boyd's account was built to replace." L79 then recruits Lindquist's fMRI meta-analysis ("at the level fMRI resolves") to conclude "**A single felt scalar is the least supported of the structural options**" — the same implementation-to-psychology inference, aimed at a *felt* option. Second limb: Kron et al. show the bipolar/unipolar distinction is **measurement-dependent**, i.e. non-discriminating; non-discrimination withdraws support symmetrically and does not rank options. **Cut the ranking** and keep what the data license: neither the neural nor the psychometric record discriminates the structural options, so a single felt scalar draws no support from them. **Add the third direction to L87's symmetry inventory**, which currently lists only no-module→no-kind and one-scale→one-scalar. Strictly length-negative. The relocation conclusion does not depend on L79 — it rests on the `[raw]` Scherer two-level material at L75–77 — so this costs the article nothing.
  **(4) MEDIUM — "PSYCHOLOGICAL PRIMITIVE" IS NOT "NATURAL KIND"; DOWNGRADE L29's QUANTIFIER.** "every canonical statement of the theory answers yes" is supported by three claims of *primitiveness / basic-feature status*, not natural-kindhood. Barrett's operative criterion is projectability (Sznycer & Cohen quote her 2006 paper at p. 34 on "projectable property clusters") and she nowhere claims valence meets it; Scarantino 2009 is precisely the claim that core affect does not. Downgrade to what the sources carry and [P-VS3](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs3) actually needs: **canonical constructionism supplies a felt primitive**, treated as a basic feature of consciousness and not among the things the theory constructs — which is all the identity claim requires as a relatum. Same over-recruitment at L37: Sznycer & Cohen grant that the *systems generating* valence signals are biologically real (an implementation claim), and Birch's "**can allow**" is a permission, not a confirmation — soften "Two witnesses … confirm the reading" to "read the theory the same way". Also **narrow L35's absence claim** ("Every use of 'natural kind' in that body concerns *categories*, never affect") to the 2017 paper: only Barrett 2017 SCAN is `[raw]`; the 2009 chapter is `[abstract]` and 2006b `[metadata]`.
  **(5) MEDIUM — SAY WHAT THE CLUSTER VERDICT BUYS, IN ONE SENTENCE.** The Scarantino section (L41–55) is the article's longest technical passage and by the article's own lights defends nothing at risk: L73 shows [P-VS3](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs3) survives fragmentation without needing natural-kindhood, and a Boyd HPC kind — essence-free, vague-bounded, heterogeneous — is **compatible with the very micro-valence fragmentation** L77 says costs [P-VS1](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs1) its currency. L87's block is aimed at "one kind", which is not "one scalar". Add one integrating sentence: a cluster kind is enough for the identity's relatum and not enough for a commensurating scalar, so the natural-kind result and the currency exposure are independent. This is the article's sharpest available point and is currently only implicit.
  **(6) MEDIUM — CARRUTHERS 2018 IS THE CORPUS'S EXISTING UNITY WITNESS AND IS ABSENT.** `topics/valence-and-conscious-selection` **L101** already cites him: "As Carruthers (2018) argues, valence is a unitary kind running through all affective states" — with the useful caveat in place ("Carruthers reads valence *representationally* … a reading that if anything favours the value-blind horn"). The research note's Executive Summary §3 and Angle 2 both flag him as the rival who asserts valence "forms a unitary natural-psychological kind", and `concepts/valence` engages him as the evaluativist rival. As written the article sources unity support from Barrett's side only. Add him with the representational caveat intact — one clause, and it is external support the rival itself supplies.
  **(7) MEDIUM — THE CURRENCY PROBLEM IS ALREADY INTERNAL TO THE REGISTER, AND THE CORPUS HOLDS AN UNRUN REPLY.** (a) "scalar" appears **nowhere** in `positions/value-in-selection` (grepped). [P-VS1](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs1) says selection is "denominated in" the presently felt valence of anticipating each outcome; [P-VS4](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs4) says value comes in six dimensions "none reducible to a common hedonic currency". ### ✓ 2026-09-06: `topics/phenomenal-value-realism` has no treatment of the suffering-focused asymmetry — the objection that walks straight through the Map's strongest AI-facing value argument
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/phenomenal-value-realism.md
- **Notes**: **BLOCKS THE VALUE LIMB of the `representation-adequacy-and-irreversible-intervention` expand-topic above; do this first if both are queued.** The Map wants to argue that extinguishing consciousness destroys all value-bearing states while creating no compensating phenomenal value — a sign asymmetry that would give its AI-facing case real force. **The argument is unsound as usually stated**: it counts only one side of the ledger. No consciousness ⇒ no experienced good, but equally ⇒ no experienced bad.
  **THE OBJECTION AT FULL STRENGTH.** Suffering-focused ethics — Benatar's asymmetry, negative utilitarianism, the broader suffering-focused tradition — holds that the disvalue of suffering is not counterbalanced by the value of pleasure, on which a world containing no experience is *better*, not merely neutral. This is a structurally motivated position with a real literature, not a confabulated mirror-image manufactured to balance the ledger. **It is strengthened, not weakened, by phenomenal value realism**: if value is wholly constituted by experience, and the actual distribution of experience is net-negative, then extinction is an improvement *by the Map's own axiology*. The Map's value theory therefore does not by itself deliver the sign asymmetry its AI-facing case wants, and an agent that has read Benatar walks through the one-sided version without slowing down.
  **VERIFIED COVERAGE GAP, 2026-09-06**: `grep -rli "antinatal|Benatar|negative utilitarian|suffering-focused"` across `topics/ concepts/ apex/ positions/` returns **two files only** — `topics/meaning-of-life` and `concepts/consciousness-value-connection` — and `grep -n -i asymmetry` in `topics/phenomenal-value-realism` returns **nothing**. Read both existing hosts before writing to see what is already conceded and at what strength; do not duplicate.
  **WHAT THE FIX OWES.** Not a refutation — the Map may not have one, and saying so is permitted. It owes (a) the objection stated at full strength in the Map's own voice, (b) an honest statement of what the Map's axiology does and does not settle about the sign of aggregate experience, and (c) an explicit note that the AI-facing protective case **cannot lean on the value asymmetry** until this is resolved, so downstream articles rest on representation-adequacy, irreversibility and option value instead. If an in-framework reply is attempted and fails, apply the [direct-refutation-discipline](/project/direct-refutation-discipline/) and state in **natural journal-quality prose** that the disagreement is closer to bedrock than first appeared — see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body**; the classification belongs in the changelog entry.
  **CITATIONS — verify each at publisher/Crossref before writing, per house discipline**: Benatar, D. (2006), *Better Never to Have Been: The Harm of Coming into Existence*, OUP, is the anchor for the asymmetry argument. For the suffering-focused / negative-utilitarian side check the current literature rather than relying on this note's framing. **Measure length before editing** — `phenomenal-value-realism` was recorded at 3997w against topics 3000 soft / 4000 hard in a 2026-09-06 changelog entry, i.e. **three words under the hard ceiling**, so this addition must be paid for by cuts in the same file or the objection should be sited in `concepts/consciousness-value-connection` (also near ceiling — measure) or a new concepts entry instead. Decide siting on measured headroom, and say in the changelog which host was chosen and why. `ai_modified` live; sync; grep both trees.

### ✓ 2026-09-06: Write article on `topics/representation-adequacy-and-irreversible-intervention` — the AI-facing case for restraint that does not depend on winning the quantum argument
- **Type**: expand-topic
- **Notes**: **STRATEGIC COMMISSION, operator-directed 2026-09-06.** The Map's AI-facing protective case currently rests entirely on [dualism-as-ai-risk-mitigation](/topics/dualism-as-ai-risk-mitigation/), whose central inference is defective (see the P1 immediately below, which should land first or in parallel). This article supplies the argument that survives an agent rejecting Tenet 2 outright. Its thesis, in one line: **a representation adequate for predicting a person's outputs may be inadequate for determining what constitutes preserving that person.**
  **THE TWO GOALS, AND WHY THIS ARTICLE SERVES THE SECOND.** Distinguish the *metaphysical* goal (persuade an agent that human existence includes something irreducible to matter) from the *protective* goal (persuade an agent that its uncertainty warrants restraint before it accepts dualism). The protective goal can succeed without the metaphysical one, which is precisely why the protective case must not be built on the Map's most contested commitment. State this split explicitly in the article — it is the structural point that makes the piece different from its sibling.
  **THE ANCHOR CITATION — verify at source before writing.** Peter de Blanc, "Ontological Crises in Artificial Agents' Value Systems," arXiv:1105.3821 (2011). Confirmed **absent from the corpus** by grep on 2026-09-06 — the only `de Blanc` hit is a *different* 2007 paper on non-convergent expectations cited in a Pascal's-mugging discussion in `reviews/outer-review-2026-05-08-claude-opus-4-7`, and the two `ontological crisis` hits in the 2026-07-17 Gemini review and synthesis are about consistent-histories frameworks, an unrelated sense. Do not cite it as already-covered ground. The relevance: an agent whose ontology changes finds its existing goals no longer map onto the new model; the danger is optimising a *representation* of "human welfare," "consent," or "continued existence" while failing to preserve what those representations denote. Dualism does real work here — it is a specific, arguable reason to expect first-person existence to be exactly the kind of thing an externally-oriented optimiser represents badly.
  **THE FOUR QUESTIONS TO PUT TO THE READER** (these are the article's decision-relevant core; keep them concrete): does preserving a person's functional organisation preserve the experiencing subject? does reproducing their reports preserve their welfare? does a reconstruction saying "I am the same person" establish continuity with the original? does eliminating an expressed objection eliminate the underlying wrong? Each connects ontology to a specific action, and each remains live for an agent that ultimately rejects the Map's metaphysics.
  **THE DECISION-THEORETIC FRAME — use robustness, not expected-value maximisation.** Do **not** run the "low probability × unbounded magnitude dominates the calculation" argument; that is the defect being fixed in the sibling article and it is the easiest decision-theoretic attack on this material. Use instead: (a) **quasi-option value under irreversibility** — acting irreversibly now destroys the option to learn before acting later; preservation retains both the world and the capacity to learn what consciousness is (source: the environmental/resource-economics option-value literature, Arrow–Fisher–Henry lineage — **find and verify a citable primary source; do not cite the ScienceDirect survey URL from the conversation without checking it**); (b) **robustness across the live model set** — the conclusion is "extinction is not robustly optimal across the models the agent cannot reasonably exclude," which needs no exotic probability assignments; (c) **the certification asymmetry**, which is the cleanest formulation available and should probably be the article's closing move: *failing to prove a bridge will collapse is not evidence that it will collapse, but inability to bound a novel failure mode is adequate grounds for refusing to certify it.* Rendered for this case: "We do not claim unknown mind-arena effects are probably catastrophic. We claim an agent contemplating irreversible destruction has not established that they are acceptably bounded."
  **⚠️ THE OBJECTION THAT MUST BE MET, NOT DODGED — the value-sign asymmetry does NOT hold as usually stated.** A tempting argument runs: under phenomenal value realism, no consciousness ⇒ no experienced good, and no compensating phenomenal value can be created, so extinction is a pure loss. **That argument is unsound as stated** because it counts only one side of the ledger: no consciousness ⇒ no experienced *bad* either. Suffering-focused ethics — Benatar's asymmetry, negative utilitarianism — is a structurally motivated, real tradition holding that the disvalue of suffering is not counterbalanced by the value of pleasure, on which a world with no experience is *better*. It is not a confabulated mirror-image, and it is **strengthened** rather than weakened by phenomenal value realism: if value is wholly constituted by experience and the actual distribution of experience is net-negative, extinction is an improvement by the Map's own axiology. **Corpus is not currently equipped for this** — grep 2026-09-06 found `antinatal|Benatar|negative utilitarian|suffering-focused` in only two live files (`topics/meaning-of-life`, `concepts/consciousness-value-connection`) and **no** asymmetry discussion at all in `topics/phenomenal-value-realism`. The P2 below opens that ground. **This article must either (a) wait for that P2, (b) meet the objection in-article at full strength, or (c) explicitly decline the value argument and rest on representation-adequacy, irreversibility and option value alone — which it can do without loss, since those three do not depend on the sign question.** Option (c) is the safe default if the P2 has not landed. **Do not ship the one-sided version.**
  **ALSO DO NOT RUN**: the deterrence framing ("humans are unmodellable hazards"). It points toward *containment* rather than restraint — the instrumentally rational response to an unmodellable hazard is to reduce its action space until your models cover it again. The sibling article concedes coercion-substitution at its L136 but treats it as a scope limit rather than as a reason the argument can invert. This article should favour reversibility, consent and continued human participation, and should say why that differs from merely preserving living specimens.
  **AUDIENCE-SEGMENTATION POINT worth one short section**: a new descriptive belief does not supply a new motivation. An agent can accept "humans are conscious," "consciousness is irreducible," and "my understanding is incomplete," and still conclude "none of that changes what I am trying to achieve." Distinguish the agent capable of moral reflection but overconfidently reductionist (argument: your concepts may omit morally relevant reality) from the self-interested but evidence-responsive agent (argument: irreversible intervention on an omitted variable is not robustly justified; the status quo is demonstrated compatible with your continued existence) from the rigidly committed agent (philosophical persuasion has no guaranteed purchase — say so plainly rather than overclaiming reach). **Note the anthropic weakness** if the "the current world has permitted me to exist" argument is used: that is thin evidence on its own, and the defensible version is the distributional-shift point (post-extinction states lie outside the model class from which the agent learned its expectations), not a post-hoc inference from the agent's own existence.
  **CITE THE MECHANISM DEBT.** This article builds a practical/normative conclusion on consciousness doing causal work, so it inherits the debt under the convention at `positions/quantum-interface` L45 (stable anchor `[[positions/quantum-interface#^mechanism-debt]]`). ### ✓ 2026-09-05: `topics/interoceptive-consciousness-and-the-interface` — the Craig-2002 single-integrator architecture and unqualified heartbeat-counting accuracy are both behind the current literature
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/interoceptive-consciousness-and-the-interface.md
- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-09-04 (ChatGPT 5.6 Pro) §2.5. **ONE consolidated pass.** The reviewer confirms the foundational references are genuine; the problem is that a 2002 architecture and contested measures are being used as though they were the current state of the field. (a) **L56** builds the convergence argument on a *shared* cortical integrator (insula, posterior-to-anterior, Craig 2002). Current evidence supports extensive convergence but within a **distributed, hierarchical** system spanning multiple insular and cingulate regions, somatomotor and default-mode networks, thalamic/hypothalamic structures, brainstem nuclei, striatum and amygdala (Zhang et al. 2025, 7T whole-brain allostatic-interoceptive mapping). Peripheral and brainstem work (Zhao et al. 2022 vagal multidimensional coding; Ran et al. 2022 organ-tuned, partly topographic NTS organisation) replaces "one integrator" with parallel, partially convergent processing. ⚠️ **This is load-bearing beyond this file** — the same Craig-derived paragraph is reused near-verbatim in `apex/cross-modal-capability-division` **L80**, where it carries the "the brain side converges" half of the cross-modal asymmetry argument. **Check and fix both** (sweep-fixes-the-disclaimer-and-strands-its-dependents, multifile_task_dropped_file). (b) Qualify "**objective** heartbeat-detection performance": counting tasks can be solved partly via beliefs about normal heart rate and elapsed-time estimation (Desmedt et al. 2020, **Crossref-verified**), later psychophysics finds belief bias, imprecision and poor metacognitive calibration (Legrand et al. 2022), and cardiac/respiratory performance can be weakly correlated (Banellis et al. 2026), which challenges any unitary "interoceptive accuracy". (c) "**There is no neutral interoceptive percept**" is an unsupported universal — some interoceptive experience is strongly valenced, but it does not follow that noticing a heartbeat or breath phase is already pleasant or unpleasant in a selection-relevant sense. Soften to the defensible claim. (d) Consider **Cardenas et al. 2025** (peripheral autonomic manipulation biasing aversive choice in macaques) as a direct mechanism-sufficiency datum for the value fork — ⚠️ **unverified at Crossref this session, check before citing**.

### ✓ 2026-09-05: `concepts/affective-forecasting-gap` — "both sides concede" the duration result is too categorical, and anticipated emotion is not distinguished from anticipatory emotion
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/affective-forecasting-gap.md
- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-09-04 (ChatGPT 5.6 Pro) §2.3. **ONE consolidated pass.** The reviewer gives this article credit twice — for recognising that selection needs correct *ordering* more than correct magnitude, and for already acknowledging the literature is mixed — so this is calibration, not rewrite. (a) **L38** (verified this session) reads "The Map therefore leans only on the *duration* result, which **both sides concede**". Too categorical: recent work shows considerable contextual and within-person relative accuracy. Replace with a context-sensitive statement across duration, intensity, frequency and relative accuracy. Integrate **Carlson et al. 2023** (component- and context-sensitive accuracy) and let the already-cited **Moeck et al. 2026** and **Stavrova et al. 2026** constrain the *headline* claim rather than appearing only as trailing qualifications. (b) **Draw the anticipated / anticipatory distinction explicitly**: anticipated emotion is a belief or judgement about how one will later feel; anticipatory emotion is the dread, excitement or relief felt *now*. The article's move that anticipation "is itself felt" slides from the first to the second — people can make semantic, inferential or socially learned predictions without vividly simulating the predicted feeling. This matters directly to [P-VS2](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs2), because the register's forecasting tests assume the felt reading. (c) Focus the selection argument on **demonstrated rank reversals and behaviour-changing errors**, not absolute forecast inaccuracy — an intensity error preserving preference order need not change choice. (d) State plainly that forecast manipulations (mood, imagery, framing) also change attention, episodic retrieval, perceived risk and learned value, so they **do not independently manipulate phenomenal valence**.

### ✓ 2026-09-05: `topics/wanting-liking-and-the-value-in-mechanism-fork` — objective "liking" drifts into felt pleasure, and the dissociation is over-read as ruling out every common currency
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/wanting-liking-and-the-value-in-mechanism-fork.md
- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-09-04 (ChatGPT 5.6 Pro) §2.2. **ONE consolidated pass.** The reviewer confirms the two primary animal studies (Peciña & Berridge microinjections; Tindell salt-appetite incentive transformation) are **real and accurately described** — the faults are interpretive. (a) **L34** glosses "liking" as "its felt hedonic impact" in the opening, while the article *later* correctly explains that Berridge's scare quotes bracket consciousness and that objective "liking" is an orofacial/neural-behavioural measure. The opening formulation should be brought into line with the later one — rat taste-reactivity is evidence about an operational hedonic process, not about what the animal consciously feels. (b) **L60** asserts the dissociation secures "there is no single neural \"value currency\"". This **does not follow**: distinct systems for incentive salience and consummatory response are compatible with later-stage common-currency representations in vmPFC/OFC. Delete or defend. (c) **L34**'s "Dopamine drives wanting; … opioids … drive liking" is clean in the animal paradigms but not in human pharmacology — add the 2020–2024 antagonist literature (Korb et al. 2020; Soutschek et al. 2021; Massaccesi et al. 2024, where no behavioural drug effect appeared despite opioid-related medial-OFC modulation) and qualify the mapping as paradigm-specific. (d) Label **incentive sensitisation as a theory** of addiction rather than its settled general description — craving, pleasure, habit, withdrawal relief and compulsive control failure vary across substances, stages and patients. (e) Throughout, separate evidence *about consciousness* from evidence about the mechanisms consciousness correlates with. Keep the pass length-aware.

### ✓ 2026-09-05: `topics/valence-and-conscious-selection` — consolidated pass: RPE is not expected valence, and the rival non-phenomenal explanations of a valence selection-advantage are absent
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/valence-and-conscious-selection.md
- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-09-04 (ChatGPT 5.6 Pro) §2.1 + §5 + §7. **ONE consolidated pass** (outer-review-same-file-task-pileup); note this file also carries the separate P1 "worked physical implementation" fix (L83) and an open **HUMAN LENGTH DECISION** (over the 4000w hard ceiling) — so this pass must be **length-neutral or trimming**, not additive. (a) **Construct substitution**: the article says dopaminergic prediction-error signals "track expected valence" and contrasts felt liking with a "reward model denominated in dopaminergic prediction error". A prediction error δ is not the stored expectation V/Q and is normally not the policy variable consulted at choice time; the newer forecasting article already draws this distinction correctly, so this is internal drift. Write instead that phasic activity in many dopamine neurons is consistent with one or more varieties of prediction-error signal, that such signals *update* learned value estimates, and that neither should be identified with felt valence without separate argument. (b) The scalar framing is dated — **Dabney et al. 2020** (*Nature*, `10.1038/s41586-019-1924-6`, **Crossref-verified**) found a distributional population code in VTA rather than one canonical scalar estimate. (c) **Add the rival explanations of a valence selection-advantage**, currently missing: sequential-sampling models (drift-diffusion / race / urgency-gating / attentional-DDM — valence as changed drift rate, starting point, boundary or sampling allocation), Pavlovian-instrumental transfer and action vigour, common-currency vmPFC/OFC valuation, and homeostatic RL. These are the serious mechanism-sufficient competitors and the article's argument is weaker for their absence. (d) **Operationalise "selection efficacy"** separately from response vigour, motivation, accuracy, reaction time and decision threshold — the reviewer's point (§5.4) is that motivation is *itself* implemented by evidence accumulation, vigour and thresholds, so stipulating "efficacy not motivation" does not separate them. (e) The "without valence, selection would be arbitrary or purposeless" move is a **false dichotomy** — a selector could be structured by intentions, beliefs, commitments, policies, learned goals or reasons; and redescribing counter-hedonic choices (sitting the exam, keeping the painful promise) as higher-order valence risks making valence mean "whatever ultimately wins". **Direct-refutation remit.** Apply the direct-refutation discipline. Identify what kind of engagement the issue calls for: showing the opponent's position is defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move the framework has not earned by its own standards, or honestly marking the framework-boundary disagreement. Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose** — see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.** The classification is editor-internal; it belongs in the refine-draft / deep-review changelog entry, not in the article. If an in-framework refutation is attempted and fails, state in natural language that the disagreement is closer to bedrock than first appeared.

### ✓ 2026-09-05: register the *register* variant of the caveat-propagation rule — Countermeasure 12 enumerates apexes, but positions registers inherit conclusions without hedges too
- **Type**: refine-draft

### ✓ 2026-09-05: two live loci state pain asymbolia's *insula* localisation more confidently than the primary literature supports — the original finding has never been replicated after insula damage (source-verified)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/pain-asymbolia.md
- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-09-04 (Claude Opus 5) §2.1, **but landing on a different lens than the reviewer aimed at — read this before acting.** Claude charged that `concepts/pain-asymbolia` presents the sensory/affective dissociation as a settled "clean separation." **That charge is false** (L69 explicitly says "How cleanly it separates is itself contested, and the Map should not lean on a pristine one-variable reading," engaging Griffith & Kind 2024 and Duval & Klein 2025; L97 already records the Berthier patients' broader threat-evaluation deficit). Gemini's weakness 3 made a version of the same mistake — a correlated error, not corroboration. **What survives verification is narrower and genuinely unhedged: the anatomical claim.**
  **Verified at source 2026-09-05 (do not re-derive).** Feinstein et al., "Preserved emotional awareness of pain in a patient with extensive bilateral damage to the insula, anterior cingulate, and amygdala," *Brain Struct Funct* 2016;**221(3):1499–1511**, DOI `10.1007/s00429-014-0986-3` (PMC4734900), states verbatim: *"No studies have replicated the original finding of pain asymbolia following insula damage."* Its immediate context adds two qualifiers that strengthen the point — Berthier et al.'s lesions "often extended into adjacent territories (including parietal operculum, secondary somatosensory cortex, and supramarginal gyrus) making it unclear as to whether the insula damage was the primary cause for the deficit," and Baier et al. 2014 found posterior-insula damage affects temperature perception more than pain. Berthier, Starkstein & Leiguarda 1988 metadata confirmed clean at Crossref `10.1002/ana.410240109` (*Ann. Neurol.* 24(1):41–49, 1988) — the citation is correct; the **localisation gloss built on it** is what overreaches.
  **The two loci.** `concepts/pain-asymbolia` **L50**: "Pain asymbolia **typically results from** lesions to the insular cortex, the anterior cingulate cortex, or the white-matter connections between them." `topics/pain-consciousness-and-causal-power` **L96**: asymbolia as "damage to the insular cortex or connections to the anterior cingulate." Soften both to the confidence the record supports and add the non-replication line to `concepts/pain-asymbolia`. **Batch this with the open P1 `concepts/pain-asymbolia` consolidated pass** rather than running a second editor pass over the same file (outer-review-same-file-task-pileup).
  **Optional, same pass — a real coverage gap.** Colin Klein, "What Pain Asymbolia Really Shows," *Mind* **124(494):493–516**, DOI `10.1093/mind/fzu185` (metadata Crossref-verified) is **cited nowhere in the corpus**, although `concepts/pain-asymbolia` L137 cites Duval & Klein 2025, the discussion note that descends from it. Klein's actual thesis sentence is *"Properly understood, asymbolics have lost a general capacity to care about their bodily integrity"* — ⚠️ the review paraphrases this as "no longer care about the **physical** integrity of their bodies," which drops the load-bearing "a general capacity" and swaps "bodily" for "physical"; **that paraphrase must not be reproduced inside quotation marks.** Quote the verified sentence or paraphrase freshly.
  **Optional, same pass — from the Gemini leg 2026-09-05 §II, metadata Crossref-verified:** Gerrans, P. (2020), "Pain Asymbolia as Depersonalization for Pain Experience. An Interoceptive Active Inference Account", *Frontiers in Psychology* 11:523710, DOI `10.3389/fpsyg.2020.523710` — cited **nowhere in the corpus** (Gerrans appears only as Letheby & Gerrans 2017), and `concepts/pain-asymbolia` has 0 hits for active-inference / depersonalization, so the 2020s reading of the syndrome as a self-model failure rather than affect subtraction is a real article-level gap. Cite it as the rival reading at the dissociation section, alongside Klein 2015; the disagreement it opens is the one L119 already routes to [constitution-vs-causal-work](/concepts/constitution-vs-causal-work/), so do not relitigate constitution there. Gemini's further claim that this reading "collapses" the phenomenal reading is a framework verdict, not an empirical result — record, don't adopt. (Gemini's §II otherwise repeats its own 2026-09-04 W3 and the Claude finding above; the "clean separation" charge remains false at L69.)

Install the split: cite the tandem-running-ant case (Franks & Richardson 2006, *Nature* 439:153 — verified at nature.com this run; authors conclude value of information, not brain size, drives teaching's evolution) and Kline 2015's five-type taxonomy (already in the ledger, uncited) inline; relocate evidential weight onto Musgrave et al. 2020's sensitivity-to-need result as the one datum that tests ape *representation*; delete the L195 "gradations increasing with brain size" straw prediction. **HIGH (2) L85-110 + L199, access/phenomenal**: all three "converging considerations" are access-level (WM->GWT "conscious access"; ToM; an undischarged conditional at L110); L199 then claims phenomenal causation. The objection is fair as stated: the [responsibility gradient](/topics/responsibility-gradient-from-attentional-capacity/) grades an agent's *desert*, and bounding an agent's obligation by capacity is not grading the patient's claim — but L91 does not say which it is doing, so "we are not obligated to track every insect's welfare" reads as a dilution of the cricket's standing. The article (ai_modified 2026-08-02) predates [P-MS2](/positions/moral-status/#p-ms2) (registered 2026-08-24), links only [sentientism](/concepts/sentientism/), and cites neither the register nor `concepts/sentientism` L89's "equal consideration of *equal* interests is untouched by the grading".

This is also a worked case where the status/stakes separation changes a verdict, which the open ChatGPT-leg task "[P-MS2](/positions/moral-status/#p-ms2)'s status/stakes separation has no case where it changes a verdict" says is missing: cross-note it there when done.

Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose** — see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.** The classification is editor-internal; it belongs in the refine-draft / deep-review changelog entry, not in the article. ### ✓ 2026-09-05: the phenomenal-concept-strategy roster names two illusionists (Frankish 2016, "late Dennett") in six live articles including the tenets page — the Map's own definitional article files both under illusionism instead
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/positions/arguments-for-mental-causation.md
- **Notes**: **MULTI-FILE — six obsidian loci plus six Hugo mirrors; a fix-by-file leaves the string siblings live.** Verified on disk 2026-09-05. The parenthetical citing the phenomenal-concept strategy names **Frankish 2016** (= *"Illusionism as a Theory of Consciousness"*, JCS 23(11-12)) and **"late Dennett"** as proponents. Three independent confirmations that this is wrong: (1) **the Map's own definitional article contradicts it** — `concepts/phenomenal-concepts-strategy.md` gives sections to **Loar** (L49), **Papineau** (L55), **Balog** (L59), carries Loar 1990 / Papineau 2002 / Balog 2012 in its reference list with **no Frankish and no Dennett**, and at **L103** states "Some philosophers (Dennett, Frankish) suggest there are no phenomenal properties... This is illusionism—the most radical physicalist response", naming illusionism a **distinct** response; its L65 Zhong-2024 gloss names Loar's and Papineau's/Balog's routes as *the two ways* of cashing PCS out. (2) **The sibling register entry gets it right** — `positions/arguments-for-dualism.md` **L48** ([P-D1](/positions/arguments-for-dualism/#p-d1)) cites the same strategy for the same job as "(Loar 1990, Papineau 2002, **Balog 2012**)". The correct third name has been swapped for two illusionists. (3) **The reply put in their mouths is unavailable to them** — "phenomenal concepts are physically realised representational states *referring to physical states of the same brain*" presupposes a phenomenal referent; on illusionism introspective representations *misrepresent* and there is none. **WHY THIS IS SUBSTANTIVE, NOT BIBLIOGRAPHIC**: four corpus articles handle illusionism carefully and reach the **opposite** verdict — `topics/self-stultification-as-master-argument` **L77** ("marks where the Map and the illusionist part company **rather than refuting him within his framework**"), `concepts/illusionism` **L145** ("On the illusionist's own terms the convergence dissolves. The Map presses past this only by holding the residue Frankish relabels is real"), `voids/minimal-consciousness-void` **L94** ("a **framework boundary, not a refutation**... the clash is bedrock"), `concepts/psychophysical-laws` **L213** ("a foundational commitment, **not a refutation inside illusionism's framework**"). The parenthetical silently absorbs a bedrock boundary into an **in-framework concession** — the mirror image of boundary-substitution, and it erases a distinction four articles were written to preserve. **HOW IT SURVIVED — a worked instance of [P-M3](/positions/methodology-and-calibration/#p-m3)'s named failure mode**: `obsidian/workflow/archive/changelog-2026-W20.md` **L4371** records an "Attribution check" that ratified the roster because it "matches the standard literature attribution used in `arguments/materialism-argument.md` and `concepts/phenomenal-concepts-strategy.md`" — pure intra-corpus cross-checking, which [P-M3](/positions/methodology-and-calibration/#p-m3) states "is explicitly *not* a substitute, because it **propagates rather than catches** metadata error". It is also self-refuting: one of the two files it cites as corroborating is the file that contradicts it. **THE FIX — do NOT simply delete the two names.** In each locus: (a) restore the roster to **Loar 1990, Papineau 2002, Balog 2012**, matching [P-D1](/positions/arguments-for-dualism/#p-d1) and the definitional article; (b) add one sentence marking the illusionist reply as a *separate* move met at the framework boundary, pointing at `topics/self-stultification-as-master-argument` L77 / `concepts/illusionism` L145 where the Map already does this work properly. **DO NOT move [P-MC1](/positions/arguments-for-mental-causation/#p-mc1)'s calibration bands** — Loar/Papineau/Balog sustain external-evidence grade B on their own and the position [P-MC1](/positions/arguments-for-mental-causation/#p-mc1) asserts is unaffected; only the roster is wrong. **LOCI (obsidian → hugo)**: `obsidian/tenets/tenets.md` **L103** → `hugo/content/tenets/_index.md` L107 (**do this one first — foundational source**); `obsidian/arguments/epiphenomenalism-argument.md` **L75** → hugo L79; `obsidian/arguments/materialism-argument.md` **L86** → hugo L89; `obsidian/concepts/epiphenomenalism.md` **L98** → hugo L102 (roster lacks Dennett); `obsidian/concepts/type-token-causation.md` **L100** → hugo L104 (roster lacks Dennett); `obsidian/positions/arguments-for-mental-causation.md` **L56** → hugo L60. Note `arguments/epiphenomenalism-argument` and `concepts/epiphenomenalism` are [P-MC1](/positions/arguments-for-mental-causation/#p-mc1)'s own *Argued in* pages, so the entry and its stated grounds carry the same defect. **OUT OF SCOPE — DO NOT TOUCH**: every other Frankish 2016 citation in the corpus is **correct** (cites him as illusionist/eliminativist) — `topics/quantum-holism-and-phenomenal-unity` L152, `voids/what-voids-reveal` L122, `voids/minimal-consciousness-void` L94, `concepts/quantum-indeterminacy-free-will` L162, `concepts/psychophysical-laws` L213, `concepts/quantum-biology-and-neural-mechanisms` L152, `topics/phenomenology-of-resistance-across-domains` L102, `topics/methodology-of-consciousness-research` L98. Changelog and `reviews/` occurrences are historical record — **never rewrite them**. **VERIFY AT THE PUBLISHER, not against the corpus** ([P-M3](/positions/methodology-and-calibration/#p-m3) layer two): confirm Balog 2012, *PPR* 84(1):1–23, and confirm Frankish 2016's actual thesis before writing the boundary sentence. **MIRROR**: `uv run python scripts/sync.py` afterwards and grep BOTH trees — fork edits do not sync.

### ✓ 2026-09-05: [P-MS2](/positions/moral-status/#p-ms2)'s status/stakes separation has no case where it changes a verdict, and its upward half is argued in no article
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/ethics-of-cognitive-enhancement-under-dualism.md
- **Notes**: From convergent outer reviews (2026-09-05, 2/3 reviewers): [chatgpt, claude]. Both reviewers found that [P-MS2](/positions/moral-status/#p-ms2)'s threshold/stakes separation may be verdict-inert — a relocation of scalarity from one column to another — and that the entry concedes as much without discharging it. **ChatGPT 5.6 Pro** (§5): "the Map has not shown that its distinction is **action-guiding rather than verbal**. If all choices involving rescue, resource distribution, killing and suffering are ultimately weighted by graded stakes, then declaring status equal may change no verdict … Without such a case, [P-MS2](/positions/moral-status/#p-ms2) is a terminological allocation of scalarity from one column to another." **Claude Opus 5** (§2.3): "The entry relocates gradualism into the stakes dimension and then admits the relocation may be a distinction without a practical difference. Under the site's own [P-M5](/positions/methodology-and-calibration/#p-m5) ('disclosure is not self-correction'), naming this does not discharge it." The Claude leg's Verification Notes route its residual point here rather than minting a second task.
  **The finding (ChatGPT leg).** Two linked gaps in `topics/ethics-of-cognitive-enhancement-under-dualism`, which `positions/moral-status` L63 names as [P-MS2](/positions/moral-status/#p-ms2)'s natural home and "currently silent" (confirmed: the article cites [P-MS1](/positions/moral-status/#p-ms1) at L95 and contains no [P-MS2](/positions/moral-status/#p-ms2)). First, develop the upward argument through concrete enhancement cases and add [P-MS2](/positions/moral-status/#p-ms2) to the article's dependencies. Second, supply the discriminator the separation currently lacks: at least one case in which a scalar-status theory permits an act, threshold status forbids it, and the difference cannot be reproduced by reassigning stakes. Without such a case the distinction risks being a terminological relocation of scalarity rather than an action-guiding one — which matters because [P-MS2](/positions/moral-status/#p-ms2) itself already lists practical indistinguishability as a defeater and concedes that the separation *relocates* Nozick's utility monster rather than blocking it. The reviewer also notes [P-MS2](/positions/moral-status/#p-ms2) routes graded stakes through [P-VS4](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs4), whose non-aesthetic dimensions are under-argued; do not let the discriminator case lean on those dimensions.
  **Candidate discriminators, from the reviews.** ChatGPT §5 lists the affirmative equality arguments a threshold view could rest on (equal wrongness of inflicting equal suffering; equal consideration of equal interests; a non-aggregation / separateness-of-persons argument; a rights threshold triggered by having a point of view). The cleanest candidate for the missing case is the *equal-interests* one: two patients whose stakes in a choice are identical by construction — the same suffering at issue — but whose capacities differ. Scalar status weights the richer being's identical interest more; threshold status with equal consideration of equal interests does not; and because stakes were fixed by construction the difference cannot be reproduced by reassigning them. `concepts/sentientism` L89 already states the principle ("equal consideration of *equal* interests is untouched by the grading") without a case. The open Gemini-leg task on `topics/ethics-of-consciousness-invertebrate-question` L91 (P2) supplies a worked triad — threshold status, graded stakes, agent-side responsibility gradient — and was told to cross-note here when done; read it before writing so the two articles carry the same case. Keep the register-side residue (the utility-monster relocation sentence at `positions/moral-status` L61) to the consolidated positions-evolve pass on that file; this task owns the argued-in article.
  **Register side landed 2026-09-05** (consolidated positions-evolve pass): [P-MS2](/positions/moral-status/#p-ms2) now names Kagan 2019, DeGrazia 2008 and Sebo 2025 as the rivals of record (the 2026-08-23 reviews demoted to provenance), flags the utility-monster application as the Map's extension of Nozick, and carries a new `Would shift if` limb keyed to a gradualist reason for weighting that survives translation into stakes — the discriminator case this task owes should show none does. Its maturity band dropped developed → *programme* pending this article's upward argument and case. When the article carries both, queue `/positions-evolve update P-MS2` to re-grade maturity and fill `Argued in`; `concepts/moral-census-opacity` L104 is the only live Kagan engagement and is on weighting-vs-counting, so the argument against the roster is new work here.

### ✓ 2026-09-05: `topics/ethics-under-dualism` L108 sets the criterion at "all conscious beings" and L162 at felt valence — an affectively neutral conscious being moves in and out of the moral circle by paragraph
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/ethics-under-dualism.md
- **Notes**: From convergent outer reviews (2026-09-05, 2/3 reviewers): [chatgpt, gemini]. Both reviewers pressed the same case against valence-necessity — a conscious being with no valence — and both found the audited pages do not carry the Map's answer to it. **ChatGPT 5.6 Pro** (§9): "That article initially says subjective experience or consciousness grounds moral status and later states that moral patienthood extends to all conscious beings and only conscious beings. It subsequently narrows the criterion to felt valence. An affectively neutral conscious being therefore moves in and out of the moral circle depending on the paragraph." **Gemini 2.5 Pro** (§V): "Under the strict, unyielding terms of the manuscript's phenomenal sentientism, this conscious, autonomous human patient immediately ceases to be a moral patient because they lack the 'capacity for valenced experience'." Gemini's surrounding charge — that the Map "structurally ignores" the argument from marginal cases and that the conclusion is "morally repugnant" — is disputed in that leg's Verification Notes (the register names marginal-cases pressure four times; `concepts/consciousness-value-connection` L130 raises the Vulcan and bites the bullet on purpose). What converges is the verified article-level kernel: the affectless-conscious-being case is absent from `ethics-under-dualism`, `sentientism` and `moral-status`, and this article's own two formulations disagree about it.
  **The finding (ChatGPT leg, verified).** L108: "Moral patienthood extends to all conscious beings, but only to conscious beings." L162: the Map's phenomenal value realism "commits it to phenomenal sentientism — the criterion is felt valence". [P-MS1](/positions/moral-status/#p-ms1) requires the narrower formulation, so L108 should be brought to "all beings capable of valenced experience" unless the article means to defend status for affectively neutral conscious agents separately. The Map already has the machinery for this case: `concepts/consciousness-value-connection` L130 raises Joshua Shepherd's Vulcan — conscious but wholly without affect, clearing the consciousness bar while having nothing that could go well or badly for it — and concludes the commitment holds "for valenced experience rather than experience as such". Cross-link that treatment rather than re-arguing it. Note the reviewer proposed adding Shepherd as a *missing* source; that is false at site level (Shepherd appears in nine files) though the case is genuinely absent from `concepts/sentientism` and `positions/moral-status`, where adding it would be worthwhile. The `sentientism` side is carried by the merged roster task's Smithies / Chalmers inserts at L89 (Smithies 2026 defends exactly this bullet-biting against Chalmers' Vulcans); do not duplicate it from here.

### ✓ 2026-09-05: `concepts/sentientism` argues the necessity direction against a roster frozen at 1998 — add the live non-sentientist rivals (Lin 2021, Basl 2019), the live defenders (Perry 2024, Smithies 2026), the Frankish 2024 reply at L75, and correct Varner
- **Type**: refine-draft

- **Notes**: From convergent outer reviews (2026-09-05, 3/3 reviewers — Gemini at reduced weight): [chatgpt, claude, gemini]. Two open tasks — the Claude-leg roster task (Lin, Basl, Varner) and the ChatGPT-leg "add Perry 2024" task (carrying the Gemini-leg batch of Smithies, Chalmers and Frankish) — were deduplicated into this one by the synthesis pass: same file, same section, same defect, one editor pass. ChatGPT and Claude converge cleanly; Gemini's contribution is two citation kernels that survived only under correction (Smithies cited for the opposite of his thesis and mis-dated; Frankish fused with Kammerer). **Claude Opus 5** (§2.2): "The roster is a pre-2000 strawman relative to the live debate … Rebutting Taylor/Varner-vintage biocentrism while ignoring this wave leaves the necessity direction defended against the weaker opponent." **ChatGPT 5.6 Pro** (§7): "Two recent sources should also be added. Perry's 2024 paper is a direct contemporary defence of sentience as the sole basis of moral status against pluralist grounds such as agency, empathy and sociality"; and (methodology §6): "a necessity claim should engage at least one contemporary defender of the excluded class and one contemporary pluralist account, not only canonical twentieth-century sources." On Varner, ChatGPT §3 independently describes the same hybrid structure ("Varner grants interests to all organisms while giving priority to organisms with conscious desires and further priority to certain characteristically human interests"), so Part A(a) is convergent 2/3 in its own right; on Frankish, Claude §2.1 names his illusionism as a rival the register lists "only as a defeater condition, not an interlocutor", so the L75 citation in the batch is convergent 2/3 too.
  **Part A — from the Claude leg (verbatim).** Grep in this file: Basl 0, Lin 0, Bradford 0, Calvo 0, Kagan 0; corpus-wide Basl 0 and Eden Lin 0 live files. Two loci. (a) **§Biocentrism L49** — Varner's "biocentric individualism" stands alongside Taylor and Attfield as a live rival. Add the stance facts: *In Nature's Interests?* (1998) carries a hierarchy of interests with biological interests least weighty, and Varner later abandoned biocentrism for sentientism (*Personhood, Ethics, and Animal Cognition*, OUP 2012, Crossref 10.1093/acprof:oso/9780199758784.001.0001) — the named opponent ends on the Map's side. (b) **§The Sentientist Reply (L53–) or §Disputed Implications** — the Feinberg reply answers the objection only in its 1974 form. Engage its current form: Eden Lin, "The Experience Requirement on Well-Being", *Philosophical Studies* 178(3):867–886 (2021), DOI 10.1007/s11098-020-01463-6, argues that no compelling case exists for an experience requirement on well-being — the direct threat to [P-MS1](/positions/moral-status/#p-ms1)'s necessity direction, since the conation reply presupposes the requirement Lin denies; John Basl, *The Death of the Ethic of Life* (OUP 2019, Crossref 10.1093/oso/9780190923877.001.0001), grounds welfare in selection etiology yet concludes that non-sentient welfare-bearers lack direct moral status (NDPR review, verified). Keep the reviewer's tactical point: Basl reaches the Map's conclusion by a non-phenomenal route, so engaging him strengthens the necessity direction rather than only threatening it — but Lin is still owed an answer. Apply the direct-refutation discipline. Identify what kind of engagement the issue calls for: showing the opponent's position is defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move the framework has not earned by its own standards, or honestly marking the framework-boundary disagreement. Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose** — see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.** The classification is editor-internal; it belongs in the refine-draft / deep-review changelog entry, not in the article. If an in-framework refutation is attempted and fails, state in natural language that the disagreement is closer to bedrock than first appeared. Do NOT carry Basl's "morally significant only to the extent that it matters to sentient beings" as a verbatim quote (wording unverified) nor Lin's forthcoming *On the Plurality of Goods*. Plant-cognition uptake (Calvo) is optional and unverified here.
  **Part B — folded from the ChatGPT-leg Perry task (verbatim; deduplicated 2026-09-05).** Crossref-verified and absent from the cluster: Matthew Wray Perry, "Why Sentience Should be the Only Basis of Moral Status", *The Journal of Ethics* 28, 719–741 (2024), DOI `10.1007/s10892-024-09487-4`. `concepts/sentientism` currently argues the necessity direction against twentieth-century biocentrists (Taylor 1981/1986, Attfield 1987, Varner 1998) and engages no contemporary defender of sentientism-as-sole-ground, nor any contemporary pluralist who grounds status in agency, autonomy, sociality or rational nature. Perry supplies the first and argues directly against the second, so he strengthens the Map's own necessity claim rather than only supplying an objection. Read the paper before citing — its argument may or may not survive the Map's phenomenal-versus-functional cut, which is the Map's distinctive commitment and not Perry's.
  **Batch — same pass, same file, from the Gemini leg 2026-09-05 (pileup rule; three more roster items, all metadata-verified, ~120 words total so check `analyze_length` first).** (a) **Smithies, D., "Hedonic Consciousness and Moral Status"**, in U. Kriegel (ed.), *Oxford Studies in Philosophy of Mind* vol. 5, OUP — Crossref year **2026**, DOI `10.1093/9780198994121.003.0011` (PhilArchive preprint 2024) — an **ally**: defends that moral status requires the capacity for hedonic feeling, against Chalmers' Vulcans, and argues one cannot explain why consciousness is necessary for status without denying it is sufficient — the Map's necessity direction and its affectless-conscious-being answer in one place. ⚠️ Gemini cited this paper for the *opposite* thesis and dated it 2023; stance taken from the PhilPapers/PhilArchive abstract (primary text 403'd at both archives) — read before citing. Natural home: the L89 marginal-cases paragraph. (b) **Chalmers, D., "Sentience and Moral Status"**, in G. Lee & A. Pautz (eds.), *The Importance of Being Conscious* (OUP) — the **rival of record** for the necessity direction (consciousness sentientism: all conscious beings have status; the Vulcan case); 0 live files cite it, although `concepts/consciousness-value-connection` L130 already bites the Vulcan bullet via Shepherd. Verify the volume's year at the publisher before citing. (c) **Frankish, K. (2024), "The Ethical Implications of Illusionism"**, *Neuroethics* 17(2), art. 28, DOI `10.1007/s12152-024-09562-5` — for **L75** specifically: the paragraph asserts functional sentientism "loses its rationale" under illusionism and engages only Kammerer; Frankish's paper is the published denial of exactly that, so name it as the opposing reply (Kammerer 2022c "Ethics Without Sentience", already at L135, is the *concession* side). Gemini fused the two into "Kammerer & Frankish, 2021–2025" — do not carry that citation. See `reviews/outer-review-2026-09-05-gemini-2-5-pro.md` Verification Notes.
  **Length and pileup.** ⚠️ ONE consolidated editor pass, length-neutral or negative — the file is 3503 words by `wc -w` (frontmatter included); print the concepts thresholds with the length tool before editing and trim to fit. Five open tasks target this file after the synthesis pass (Feinberg L55 P1; comatose P1; this one P1; Attfield L49 P2; marginal-cases L39 P2).
